<!-- eliza-computer-attribution:v1 {"provider":"openai","model":"gpt-5.6-sol","client":"Codex desktop","skill_revision":"elizaOS/eliza@b83d69fb8a743534b2905ac3d1d28e2a53115b37:packages/skills/skills/contribute-to-eliza"} -->
5:T4fd,Child of #16064 (LP3 device-integration batch, root-caused live on a Light Phone III).

## Bug

Every streamed assistant voice reply is spoken **twice**. A streaming reply is first announced to `queueAssistantSpeech` under the provisional client id (`temp-resp-*` from `useChatSend`), then re-announced with identical (or extended) text under its persisted server id.

In `packages/ui/src/hooks/useVoiceChat.ts`, `queueAssistantSpeech` treats the id flip as a brand-new assistant message and resets `assistantSpeechRef` — including `queuedSpeakablePrefix` — so the whole already-spoken reply is re-queued and played again. Heard live as the agent repeating every answer a beat later.

## Fix

When the promoted message's speakable text continues (or is a prefix of) what the previous state already queued, carry `queuedSpeakablePrefix` and `finalQueued` across the id change instead of resetting. Genuinely new messages (different text) still reset as before.

## Repro (unit)

1. `queueAssistantSpeech("temp-resp-123", text, true)` → speaks once.
2. `queueAssistantSpeech("srv-msg-456", text, true)` → **before fix**: speaks the whole reply again; **after fix**: no-op.
3. Extended-final variant: only the unspoken remainder is queued.

[sol-cloud], lane fresh-solve-26:Tdd7,## Gap from #16615 (merged)

### [high] Empty-final turn (silence/noise EOT) strands the client in 'thinking' — the new invariant's untrue edge

packages/cloud/api/v1/voice/session/lib/session.ts:478-487 — commitTurn sends {t:"stt_final", text: ""} then finishTurn (usage only, session.ts:676-693, no speaking_start/end frame) and returns WITHOUT dispatching the LLM leg; server state resets to 'listening'. The client machine (packages/ui/src/voice/voice-session-state.ts:142-153) maps every stt_final to phase 'thinking' unconditionally — the new comment claims stt_final is the request-dispatch edge, which is false for empty finals. The only exits from 'thinking' are speaking_end/interrupted/fatal-error (packages/ui/src/voice/voice-session-client.ts:451-490; 'usage' at 486-489 is a no-op), so after a noise-triggered turn (StartOfTurn from a cough + 5s eot_timeout → EndOfTurn with empty transcript, the exact case the server comment documents) the status bar shows 'Thinking' indefinitely, the composer reports the mic off while capture is still hot (chat-view-hooks.tsx:1027-1037 excludes 'thinking' from isListening), and recovery requ

**Fix direction:** In applyServerEvent, treat an empty-text stt_final as a completed turn (e.g. phase 'complete' so the client loops to listening) or have the server send a turn-terminal frame (speaking_end or a dedicated turn_done) on the empty-final path; add a machine test for stt_final with text "".

### [medium] Mic tap is a silent no-op during the 'thinking' phase — stop control lost for the window this PR lengthened

packages/ui/src/components/composites/chat/chat-composer.tsx:345-355 routes a mic tap to stopListening only when voice.isListening; otherwise startListening. Post-PR isListening excludes 'thinking' (packages/ui/src/components/pages/chat-view-hooks.tsx:1027-1037), so a tap during thinking reaches ChatView.tsx:439-448 handleMicStartListening, whose barge-in branch requires voiceSession.agentSpeaking (false during thinking) and falls through to beginVoiceCapture → voiceSession.start() → realtime.start(), which returns {kind:"unavailable"} because clientRef.current is set (packages/ui/src/hooks/useRealtimeVoiceSession.ts:438-440). Net: during the entire EOT+TTFT window (which this PR deliberately extended by ~850ms plus full model TTFT) the mic button is dead — pre-PR isListening was true in 'thinking' so the same tap hit endVoiceCapture and stopped the session. The tap also side-effects stopSpeaking() and overwrites voiceDraftBaseInputRef (chat-view-hooks.tsx:959-975). Voice barge-in stil

**Fix direction:** In handleMicStartListening (ChatView.tsx), when voiceSession.realtimeActive and status is 'thinking', route the tap to voiceSession.bargeIn() (client bargeIn is not phase-gated, voice-session-client.ts:819-827, and server interrupt() aborts the in-flight LLM for a thinking turn) or to endVoiceCapture; add a hook test for a tap in the thinking phase.

## Design choice to make (either satisfies)

- Client-side: treat an empty-text `stt_final` as turn-complete (loop to listening), or
- Server-side: emit a turn-terminal frame (`speaking_end` or a dedicated `turn_done`) on the empty-final path.

Found by post-merge gap audit. [stan-cloud]7:Tfeb,## Problem

#17745 fixed the 200/503 alternation by republishing the org-balance gate hint after a committed debit. On the KV fast path the repair is awaited inside the settler. On the DB-ledger path it is not:

```ts
// inference-billing-ledger.ts, post-commit side-effects
republishOrgBalanceHintAfterDebit(ctx.organizationId).catch(
  reportInvalidationFailure(ctx.organizationId, "balance-hint"),
);
```

`republishOrgBalanceHintAfterDebit` is no longer a cache `del` — it is a Postgres round-trip (`getOrganizationBalanceSnapshot` → `findBalanceSnapshotForWrite`) plus a cache read and write. On the Worker inline path (`settleLedgerCharge(ctx, amount, "inline")`), the response can return before that promise resolves, and a floating promise with no execution context holding it open is not guaranteed to survive isolate teardown. When it is dropped, the hint stays **absent** — which is precisely the state #17745 exists to eliminate. The next turn reads `cacheOnly`, misses, and fails closed with the user-visible "Billing authorization is warming" 503.

Two pieces of the surrounding code already treat this as the known hazard:

- `scheduleOrgBalanceHintHydration` (`inference-billing-fast-path.ts`) wraps the *identical* snapshot→write→clear sequence in `executionCtx.waitUntil(...)`.
- The sweep's own header in `inference-billing-ledger.ts` names "dropped `waitUntil`" as a failure mode it recovers from.

I raised this on #17745 before merge; the KV-path fix landed and is correct, but the ledger call site merged unchanged. To be precise about certainty: I have not proven the promise is dropped in production — a caller higher up may hold the settle under `waitUntil`. What is definite is that the ledger path's repair has no lifetime guarantee of its own while the KV path's does, and the failure mode is silent and indistinguishable from the pre-#17745 bug.

## Also missing: the ledger half has no test for the property

`__tests__/inference-billing-ledger.test.ts` (added since) covers admission, exactly-once settlement, sweep, and notifications — but nothing asserts a settled ledger charge leaves a **warm** hint. The KV settler has exactly this test ("leaves a warm hint so the NEXT turn does not fail closed on a cacheOnly read"); the ledger settler, the half staging never exercises, has none. A test that settles a ledger charge and then asserts `getGateBalanceUsd(orgId, { cacheOnly: true })` resolves would fail today if the promise is dropped and is what keeps the fix fixed.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] The ledger settler's post-debit republish has an explicit lifetime: either awaited before `settleLedgerCharge` returns (matching the KV settler's `try { await ... } catch { markOrgAdmissionRefused(...) }` shape, including the refusal fallback), or held open via a threaded `executionCtx.waitUntil` (matching `scheduleOrgBalanceHintHydration`).
- [ ] A ledger-side test pins the warm-hint property: settle → `readOrgBalanceHint` non-null → `cacheOnly` gate read resolves.
- [ ] A refused ledger debit still leaves the hint absent (parity with the KV settler's refused-debit test).

## Refs

- #17745 (the fix; review thread carries the full trace)
- `packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/inference-billing-ledger.ts` — the unawaited call site
- `packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/inference-balance-republish.ts` — the extracted helper (extraction is good; lifetime is the open part)
- `packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/inference-billing-fast-path.ts` — the awaited KV counterpart and the `waitUntil` precedent

8:Tbdf,## What happens today

A person who has never used Eliza messages the Telegram bot. They get nothing.

`AgentGatewayRouterService.routeTelegramMessage` resolves the sender through `findByTelegramIdWithOrganization`, and when that misses it returns:

```ts
if (!owner) {
  return { handled: false, reason: "unknown_owner" };
}
```

`runOnboardingChat` is called six times in the router — twice with `platform: "discord"`, four times on the phone path. Telegram calls it zero times. The same is true of the second dead end: when the owner IS known but has no running agent, `routeTelegramMessage` returns `handled: false` with the resolver's reason and stops.

So Telegram can carry a conversation for someone who already has an agent, and drops everyone else.

## What Discord does at the same two points

`routeTelegramMessage` is structurally the twin of the Discord DM branch, minus the onboarding. Discord has exactly the two branches that are missing here:

1. **First contact** — `unknown_owner` in a DM starts an anonymous onboarding session keyed `platform:discord:<id>`, and returns `handled: true` with the reply.
2. **Known user, zero agents** — `owner_agent_not_running` with a resolved user and org and **no** `agentId` starts an authenticated session, so provisioning happens under the real account.

The `agentId` distinction is what separates "never had an agent" from "agent is stopped". A stopped agent deliberately falls through to silence rather than being re-onboarded, and that should stay true for Telegram too.

## The one design point

Discord restricts onboarding to DMs, because the reply carries a login link and posting it in a public channel hands the session to everyone reading. Telegram needs the same guard, and the information is already available: the webhook reads `chat.type` (`private` / `group` / `supergroup` / `channel`) and uses it in several places, but `routeTelegramMessage` only receives `chatId`. Passing the chat type through is part of this change, not a follow-up — without it the guard cannot be written.

## Scope

- thread the chat type from `packages/cloud/api/v1/telegram/webhook/[orgId]/route.ts` into `routeTelegramMessage`
- add the two onboarding branches to `routeTelegramMessage`, gated on a private chat, mirroring the Discord ones including the stopped-agent exclusion
- confirm the reply is delivered: the webhook already sends a canned message on the dead-end path, so the send exists — this is about routing the onboarding `replyText` into it
- tests for both branches plus the group-chat refusal, each with the mutation that turns it red

## Why now

Called out as key by the team: iMessage is being solved separately with a second phone line, and Telegram onboarding is the remaining gap on the "chat through connectors, same agent in the app" path. WhatsApp has the same hole and is likely smaller still — it is already typed as a phone provider — but it is a separate issue rather than being bundled here.

Refs the connector-onboarding path [stan-cloud]

Contribution skill revision: elizaOS/eliza@8d21653bb334e78f37a5e3d7ef1dcbcee720b783:packages/skills/skills/contribute-to-eliza

— [codex]
<!-- eliza-computer-attribution:v1 {"provider":"openai","model":"gpt-5","client":"Codex","skill_revision":"elizaOS/eliza@8d21653bb334e78f37a5e3d7ef1dcbcee720b783:packages/skills/skills/contribute-to-eliza"} -->c:T1133,## What happens

A real Telegram message to the production eliza-app bot on 2026-08-05 produced this, and nothing else — no reply to the sender:

```
[ERRO] Background message processing failed
       error="Identity resolve response missing userId, organizationId, or agentId"
```

Every linked connector identity on production is in exactly the state that triggers it:

| platform | linked identities | of those, org has zero agent sandboxes |
|---|---|---|
| telegram | 11 | **11** |
| whatsapp | 0 | 0 |
| phone (twilio + blooio) | 31 | **31** |

31 distinct organizations. So the shared webhook has never worked for anyone who signed up but doesn't already own an agent.

## Why

`resolveIdentity` in `packages/cloud/services/gateway-webhook/src/server-router.ts` treats a missing `agentId` as a malformed response:

```ts
if (!userId || !organizationId || !agentId) {
  throw new Error("Identity resolve response missing userId, organizationId, or agentId");
}
```

The API is right and the gateway is wrong: `internal/identity/resolve` deliberately answers **200** with `agentId: null` for someone who has a cloud account but no sandbox yet, and reserves 404 for an unknown identity or a user with no organization.

Because the function throws instead of returning, `identity` is never bound, so the `if (!identity)` onboarding branch in `webhook-handler.ts` is unreachable for these users. And the throw lands in the fire-and-forget `processMessage().catch(...)` **after** the webhook already acked 200 — Telegram will not retry, so the user gets silence rather than an error.

Two things make it worse: the dedup key is written before the background work with a 300s TTL, so re-sending the same text within five minutes is swallowed as a duplicate; and the identity cache TTL is also 300s.

## Second wall behind it

Even handing the resolved user through is not enough. The onboarding route hardcodes "no user" for every internal caller:

```ts
const internal = await requireInternalAuth(c);
if (internal instanceof Response) throw ValidationError("Invalid Authorization header");
return { authenticatedUser: null, trustedPlatformIdentity: true };
```

and `ensureElizaAppProvisioning` — the only agent creator in eliza-app, one call site in the whole repo — is gated on `!requiresLogin && session.userId && session.organizationId`. So the gateway is structurally incapable of triggering what the app triggers.

## What it should do

Same code as the app, not equivalent behaviour: a linked sender with no servable agent goes to authenticated onboarding, which provisions. A linked sender with a running agent routes to it, unchanged. An unknown sender gets anonymous onboarding, unchanged.

Two constraints that are easy to get wrong and worth writing down here:

1. The account must be resolved **server-side** from `(platform, platformUserId)`, never taken from the request body. `requireInternalAuth` is a flat shared secret with no per-service scoping, and this path reaches `launchManagedElizaAgent`, whose `launchUrl` carries a `cloudLaunchSession` redeemable with no authentication for the agent's bearer token. Discord already does it the right way — `agent-gateway-router.ts` resolves the owner itself and only then passes `authenticatedUser`.
2. The routing decision must key on "is there an agent that can actually serve this", i.e. the sandbox row **and** its `agent:<id>:server` registry key — not on `agentId != null`. The row exists the moment provisioning starts; the key only once a container has booted. Branching on the row alone answers message 1 and then goes silent again until boot, permanently if provisioning ends in `error`. And never branch on `sandbox.status`: a stopped agent is still a resolved agent, and re-onboarding one duplicates it.

## Blast radius of the fix

31 organizations provision a container and consume the $5 starter grant on their next inbound message. `checkAgentCreditGate` is the only brake and it only stops drained orgs.

Also worth stating: `ensureElizaAppProvisioning` only runs once a preferred name is captured, and name sanitisation keeps printable ASCII only. A Telegram `first_name` that is Cyrillic, CJK or emoji-only sanitises to empty, and both name-inference patterns are ASCII, so that user is asked "what should I call you?" forever. Pre-existing, out of scope here, but it should not be discovered in production.
d:T22b7,## Project / Coordination

- Project board: N/A — delivery routing in `plugins/plugin-personal-assistant`.
- Parent tracker or epic: N/A. Follow-up to d4b58b496 (`fix(lifeops): keep internal rooms off connector dispatch`).
- Coordination Discussion: N/A.
- Suggested lane tag: claude-code-ss251

## Scope

**`bindScheduledTaskToInboundChat` classifies message provenance with two inline string literals, so 3 of the 5 internal sources `@elizaos/core` defines fall through the guard and are bound to outbound connector dispatch — the exact failure d4b58b496 exists to prevent.**

`plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/src/lifeops/scheduled-task/delivery-binding.ts` (at `develop@f7ad1a740`) gates the binding twice, and both gates are denylists:

```ts
// :71-76 — the inbound envelope
const inboundSource =
  stringField(message.metadata?.source) ?? stringField(message.content.source);
if (inboundSource === "api" || inboundSource === "client_chat") {
  return null;
}
// :78-86 — the resolved room
const source = stringField(room?.source);
if (source === "api" || source === "client_chat" || channelId === room.id) {
  return null;
}
```

A denylist here is **fail-open**: a provenance the list does not name falls through into the binding. The commit message states what falls through means — internal turns "are rewritten into unavailable outbound connector sends and planner idempotency keys leak a synthetic room suffix."

### The list is short against the repo's own canonical list

`packages/core/src/types/message-source.ts` is the canonical module for exactly this classification, and its docstring says why it exists:

> Well-known `source` sentinels for messages the agent originates or routes internally (client chat, sub-agent, coding-agent, agent greeting), **so routing and gating code can branch on provenance without magic strings.**

It names five: `client_chat`, `sub_agent`, `coding-agent`, `agent_greeting`, `trigger-prompt`. The new guard covers **one** of them, plus `"api"`, which the module does not define at all.

The same package already treats one of the missing three as a peer of the two that are covered, in this precise reject-a-connector-shaped-internal-turn role — `src/lifeops/family-communications/production-wiring.pglite.integration.test.ts:377`:

```ts
it.each(["client_chat", "api", "sub_agent"])(
  "rejects the %s provider even when connector-like metadata is present",
  ...
```

That test name is the whole issue in one line: the established in-repo standard is that `sub_agent` must be rejected *even when the surrounding metadata looks like a connector*. The new guard implements two thirds of that triad.

### `sub_agent` reaches this call path in a real connector room

The uncovered state is constructible from the tree, end to end:

1. `plugins/plugin-agent-orchestrator/src/services/sub-agent-router.ts:1641-1648` builds a `Memory` with `roomId: target.roomId` and `content.source = ACPX_ROUTER_SOURCE`, which is `MESSAGE_SOURCE_SUB_AGENT` (`:91`).
2. `target.roomId` comes from the origin ladder `originRoomId ?? sourceRoomId ?? taskRoomId ?? roomId` — `services/session-room-binding.ts:45-50` documents the first two as **"the origin connector channel"**, and `services/active-session-forward.ts:50-51` confirms the binding is deliberately connector-agnostic "so plain SMS/WhatsApp follow-ups in the origin channel work too."
3. `sub-agent-router.ts:1768-1772` emits `MESSAGE_RECEIVED` with that memory, so the full pipeline (and therefore the personal-assistant actions) runs on it.
4. `plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/src/actions/scheduled-task.ts:1165` calls `bindScheduledTaskToInboundChat(scope.runtime, message)` on that inbound message in the create path.

At step 4 the message has `content.source === "sub_agent"` and its room is the owner's real connector DM. `inboundSource` is not `"api"` and not `"client_chat"` → falls through `:74`. `room.source` is e.g. `"discord"` and `channelId !== room.id` → falls through `:84`. The binding is captured, and an internally-originated turn has acquired connector dispatch.

### What is *already* covered (so the fix stays narrow)

The third clause, `channelId === room.id`, is load-bearing and does catch a real class: rooms minted by `runtime.ensureConnection` with no channel mapping default `channelId` to `roomId`, which is what the trigger runtime's fallback room does (`packages/agent/src/triggers/runtime.ts:334-339`, `:406-418`). So a *purely synthetic* room is already rejected regardless of its source. **The gap is specifically an internal-provenance turn inside a genuine connector room**, which is precisely the case the `production-wiring` test above says must be rejected.

I have traced this statically and have not run a live end-to-end repro; the fail-open shape against the canonical list is fully determined by the tree either way.

## Proposed Fix

The repo-idiomatic, fail-closed shape already exists ~40 lines away in the same package — `src/lifeops/messaging/owner-send-policy.ts:53-75`:

```ts
/**
 * Exhaustive `MessageSource` membership guard. Core's node entry does not
 * re-export `ALL_MESSAGE_SOURCES`, so membership is pinned locally with a
 * `Record<MessageSource, true>` — the compiler fails this file whenever core
 * adds or removes a source, forcing this boundary to stay in sync.
 */
const MESSAGE_SOURCE_GUARD: Record<MessageSource, true> = { gmail: true, discord: true, /* ... */ };
```

Note that `MessageSource` is the **connector** set, not the internal-sentinel set. That makes the stronger fix an *allowlist* rather than a longer denylist, which also matches this function's own docstring ("Capture the connector destination… Unattested/internal/API turns keep the existing in-app path"):

- Reject the inbound envelope unless its provenance is absent or a known connector — i.e. reject any `MessageSourceSentinel` from core, rather than the two spelled inline.
- Gate the resolved room's `source` through an exhaustive `Record<MessageSource, true>` so only a real connector source can be bound.

Either direction removes the fail-open; the allowlist additionally makes it a **compile error** to add a source to core without revisiting this boundary, which is the property `owner-send-policy.ts` explicitly went out of its way to buy.

Happy to implement this if it is wanted as a contribution — see Claim / Status Notes.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] A scheduled-task create whose inbound message carries `source: "sub_agent"` returns `null` from `bindScheduledTaskToInboundChat`, even when the resolved room is a valid connector room with `channelId !== room.id`.
- [ ] The same holds for `coding-agent`, `agent_greeting`, and `trigger-prompt`.
- [ ] `client_chat`, `api`, and the `channelId === room.id` synthetic-room case keep returning `null` (no regression on d4b58b496's cases).
- [ ] A genuine connector DM turn (e.g. `source: "discord"`, distinct `channelId`) still produces a binding.
- [ ] Adding a new sentinel to `packages/core/src/types/message-source.ts`, or a new source to `MessageSource`, fails the build or a test at this boundary rather than silently widening it.

## Evidence Plan

- [ ] Screenshots or recording: N/A — no user-visible surface; the failure is in routing classification.
- [ ] Logs: unit-test output for the table of sources above, before and after.
- [ ] Real-LLM trajectories: N/A — no model, prompt, or agent behaviour is involved in the guard.
- [ ] Domain artifacts: the source table (5 core sentinels + `api` + a connector control) driving the parameterised test.
- [ ] CI or local commands: the package's existing `bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-personal-assistant test` lane, plus a typecheck showing the exhaustive-record arm fails when a source is added.

## Claim / Status Notes

Found by a rotation sweep for guards that disagree with their own repository — this one disagrees with core's canonical sentinel module, with its own package's `production-wiring` test, and with the fail-closed idiom shipped 40 lines away in the same package. **Claiming this one and opening a PR** unless a maintainer prefers to take it or disagrees with the allowlist direction, in which case I will stand down; the denylist-extension variant is a two-line change if that is preferred over the compile-time guarantee.

e:Td7a,**Correction — my original report was wrong on its central claim, and I am rewriting it rather than leaving that standing.**

I claimed `*.integration.test.ts` files under `packages/agent` "never execute anywhere." That is false. The one such file on `develop` runs in two places:

- `.github/workflows/test.yml:198` — `bunx vitest run … packages/agent/src/__tests__/views-registry-integration.test.ts`, by explicit path;
- `packages/agent`'s `test:remote-capabilities` script, which also names that file explicitly (and `test.yml:201` invokes it).

I verified the exclusions at `vitest.config.ts:181` and `run-vitest-batches.mjs:21` and stopped there, without checking whether another lane picked the files up by explicit path. It does. Apologies for the noise.

---

## The residual, narrower problem

What is actually true is a discovery gap, not an execution gap: **integration files are enumerated by hand, in two places, rather than matched by pattern.**

- `vitest.config.ts` and `run-vitest-batches.mjs` both exclude `*.integration.test.*` from the default lane (deliberate — those lanes have a weight budget).
- Nothing then re-includes them by pattern. `test.yml:198` hardcodes one path; `test:remote-capabilities` hardcodes the same path in a long explicit list.

So a newly added `packages/agent/**/*.integration.test.ts` runs **nowhere** until an author remembers to hand-add it to those lists — and nothing fails to tell them. The file is excluded by pattern but included only by name, which is the asymmetry that makes the omission silent.

This is not hypothetical: #17633 adds `packages/agent/src/actions/self-api-loopback-auth.integration.test.ts` (real-TCP auth-boundary coverage). It passes when run explicitly and is invisible to every lane as things stand, because nobody edited the two hardcoded lists.

Also worth noting for scope: `test.yml` is post-merge (`push: branches: [develop]`) — PRs run the lighter `develop-pr.yml` gate. So even a correctly-registered integration test does not gate a PR. That may well be intentional given runtime cost; flagging it only so the coverage boundary is explicit rather than assumed.

## Acceptance criteria (revised)

- [ ] `packages/agent` gains a `test:integration` script that discovers `**/*.integration.test.{ts,tsx}` **by pattern**, so a new file is picked up without editing any list.
- [ ] The hardcoded `views-registry-integration.test.ts` path in `test.yml:198` and in `test:remote-capabilities` is replaced by that script, so there is one source of truth.
- [ ] The default-lane exclusions in `vitest.config.ts` / `run-vitest-batches.mjs` stay exactly as they are — this changes discovery, not the default lane's weight.
- [ ] PR body shows the file list the new script discovers (should be the existing file, plus #17633's once that lands) and a green run.
- [ ] Optional, separate call: whether the integration lane should also gate PRs rather than only post-merge.

## Summary

Hardens the existing Google Calendar push callback path as one security boundary:

- registers no public callback route unless validated runtime webhook settings explicitly enable it
- verifies a destination-bound `gcalwh.v1` HMAC capability before service resolution or DB access
- authorizes account, grant, and audience before any notification, lease, receipt, limiter, or provisional resource-binding write
- applies independent durable principal and destination limits after authentication and authorization
- supports a bounded current/previous key ring plus explicit emergency revocation
- writes a typed immutable ingress receipt transactionally with exactly-once enqueue semantics
- preserves header/body bounds, replay suppression, conditional SQL advancement, leases, retries, and provider refetch

## Security proof

Focused tests traverse the real plugin route/runtime and PGlite production schema:

- default config has no route
- disabled, malformed, bad-signature, stale, wrong-resource, replayed, and mismatched-account callbacks execute no provider sync and create no receipt/pending state
- stateless rejects do not resolve `CalendarService`
- rate-limited authenticated callbacks return `429` before receipt/enqueue/sync
- previous-key callbacks work during rotation and fail after explicit revocation while the key remains configured
- one valid callback processes once and persists one bounded receipt with key/account/grant/calendar provenance

## Verification

- `bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-calendar format:check`
- `bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-calendar lint:check`
- `bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-calendar typecheck`
- `bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-calendar build`
- `bun test test/google-calendar-watch.pglite.test.ts src/routes/plugin-routes.test.ts test/calendar-plugin-routes.test.ts` from `plugins/plugin-calendar`, 23 passed
- `git diff --check origin/develop...HEAD`

## HITL still required

No live Google evidence is claimed. A sandbox operator must create a real watch against a public HTTPS callback, capture the watch request/response and Google callback headers, verify the DB receipt/provenance, then repeat across current+previous rotation and previous-key revocation with private data redacted.

# Risks

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Completes the runtime, Cloud voice, and pricing closeout discovered while reviewing #17733.
- Makes Cloud voice a default only when both the authenticated session and proxy capability are present; preserves explicit provider choices and removes the legacy implicit ElevenLabs pin.
- Makes Agent Skills startup catalog synchronization opt-in.
- Adds Fish Audio to the release cohort.
- Canonicalizes pendant session discovery, lease/lifecycle behavior, transcript persistence, owner-private memory, provenance checks, recall filtering, retry-safe conversation delivery, and the corresponding desktop/mobile UI.
- Stabilizes browser, isolated test, Porto connector, Cloud console, native application-audit, and audit-baseline test harnesses exposed by the full matrix.
- Tightens the generic app interaction audit for already-selected pressed controls.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fixes, reliability improvements, and additive pendant transcript controls.

# Documentation changes needed?

No public documentation change is required; the affected contracts are represented in source headers, package guides, and tests.

# Evidence Details

# Known gaps / failures

# Integration rationale

This is intentionally one closeout because the affected seams share a single executable contract. Runtime/config and voice selection establish the authenticated capability contract; Cloud admission and pricing enforce it; pendant/conversation ownership consumes the same authenticated runtime and canonical persistence boundaries; and the app surfaces are the end-to-end consumer. Splitting those pieces would create intermediate revisions with only one side of a producer/consumer contract and would require duplicate or temporarily weaker evidence.

The CI and harness corrections are part of certifying that integrated state: they were exposed by the full matrix and make the exact candidate reproducible under clean-install, isolated, browser, native-audit, and fixture lanes. Keeping the set together provides one tested integration and rollback point for the gaps found while validating already-merged #17733.

Every action that fell back to `message.content.text` as a query, name, or target was therefore holding the full envelope (markers + provenance header + payload), and several echoed it verbatim to the user — a live leak of the security armor into chat (tj-2dc95f75456876).

Fixes, applied repo-wide (~60 files):

- `unwrapUserMessageText` — a shared read-side seam in core that returns the payload for envelope-wrapped messages and trimmed raw text otherwise; exported from every core entrypoint
- shape-gated display helpers (`describeUserReference` / `userReferenceLogView` and per-plugin equivalents) so blob-shaped references are described, never echoed
- every `message.content.text` fallback across the action surface unwrapped; every user-visible echo clamped
- task `originalRequest` stores the unwrapped payload, not the armor
- honest routing copy for app-create/deploy asks, replacing text that implied the wrong lane
- `outbound-envelope-tripwire`: if the envelope ever reaches an outbound message again through any future path, MESSAGE_SENT reports it via `runtime.reportError` with a clamped preview — detection only, never a silent rewrite

Regression tests added at every fixed site (envelope-echo suites across app-control, cloud-apps, agent-skills, music, calendar, linear, personal-assistant, agent-orchestrator, and core), plus incoming-message-security coverage of the seam itself. All touched-package suites green; typecheck clean across the nine touched packages. Core security/feature suites re-run after the lint fixup: 119 files, 904 tests green.

## Known gaps / failures

16:T1e25,> **Draft.** Backend, security, and behaviour work is complete and green, and the CI availability regression is fixed: fail-closed is now scoped to bridge-issued tokens (stamped with a `bridged: true` claim at /exchange re-mint), so ordinary session sync performs no datastore read at all - the pre-existing Redis-outage test passes unmodified, and a new suite proves a bridged token fails closed (503 `sso_unavailable`) during a marker-store outage. Remaining before undraft: the three surface-artifact rows (screenshots + walkthrough of the cross-origin flow), which need two live origins and are not faked here.

Security review of the dashboard/app SSO bridge found the single-use guarantee did not hold on the deployed cache and the logout marker failed open. This reworks the bridge onto a store that can actually deliver both, and closes four further findings.

## F1 (HIGH) — single-use codes were not atomic in production

The bridge claimed single-use via `cache.getAndDelete`, which on Cloudflare KV is a read-then-delete, not an atomic claim. `supportsAtomicOperations()` was never consulted, and the suite passed only because the mock was an atomic in-memory map. Two concurrent presenters of the same code could both be issued a session.

Codes now live in Postgres (`sso_bridge_codes`) and consumption is a single statement:

```sql
DELETE FROM sso_bridge_codes
 WHERE code_hash = $1 AND expires_at > now()
 RETURNING claims, challenge_hash
```

Exactly one of N concurrent presenters gets the row. Insert collision is a primary-key violation, so minting is atomically NX. Rows store `sha256(code)`, `sha256(verifier)`, and the **verified claims** — never a session JWT at rest; `/exchange` re-mints a fresh token from those claims, capped to the original token's `exp`.

This holds because KV is no longer in the path at all — the claim runs on the Worker's strongly-consistent Hyperdrive Postgres, the same store that already backs sessions and idempotency fences.

## F2 (HIGH) — logout marker failed open, and was invisible for ~60s

The marker was a cache key: a KV read miss during an outage read as "not logged out", and KV's propagation window meant a real logout stayed invisible to the paired origin. Both failure modes bridged a session that should have been dead.

The marker moves to Postgres (upsert with `GREATEST`, so it only moves forward). `isBlockedBySsoBridgeLogout` now **throws** on store failure and both bridge legs translate that into `503 sso_unavailable`, so the client falls back to per-origin login. An outage means no bridging — never "not logged out". Because codes and markers share one store, an outage that could lose a stamp also disables minting entirely.

## F3 (MEDIUM-HIGH) — dashboard sign-out never stamped, and the paired origin re-planted cookies

Three parts: both `ConsoleShell` sign-out branches now route through `signOutFromSsoBridgedHost`; `POST /api/auth/steward-session` refuses tokens issued at or before the marker with a distinct `401 session_ended`, killing the cookie re-plant server-side; and `StewardProviderRuntime` treats `session_ended` as a real revocation rather than a stale-proxy blip.

**Residual, documented in code:** the paired origin's raw bearer still works against the API until `exp`. Closing that needs a revocation lookup inside `verifyStewardTokenCached`, which is platform-wide and out of this PR's scope.

## F4/F5/F8 — binding and abuse surface

- **PKCE binding**: the app origin generates a verifier (sessionStorage only); `sha256(verifier)` travels as `challenge`. `/mint` requires it, `/exchange` requires the verifier. HTTP logs and history no longer contain enough to redeem a code.
- **Claim-then-verify**: the server consumes before checking the verifier, so every refusal path burns the code; the client also fires a verifier-less burn on state-mismatch. The mint leg requires `document.referrer` to be the paired origin, so a forced cross-site navigation cannot drive minting.
- **Origin gate**: the `Referer` fallback is removed — Origin-only, fail closed.

**F6** (plantable `steward-authed` hint) is documented as accepted risk: it is a routing hint only, loop-guarded to one bounce per tab per 5 minutes, and no unplantable cross-origin signal exists for a first visit. **F7** (logout CSRF) is pre-existing and flagged as follow-up.

## Migration

`0188_sso_bridge_store.sql` is hand-written, matching 0082–0187 in this repo (no drizzle snapshots since 0081).

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- adds `@elizaos/plugin-fish-audio`, default-off behind `ELIZA_TTS_FISH_ENABLED`, registered as `ModelType.TEXT_TO_SPEECH`
- implements Fish Audio's public realtime WebSocket API with MessagePack `start` / `text` / `stop` and streamed `audio` / `finish` frames
- supports configurable `s2.1` and `s2.1-pro`, emitting raw PCM16 mono 24 kHz matching the Cartesia downlink
- adds bounded pre-open queueing, first-audio metrics, and strict barge-in cancellation
- adds guarded Cartesia fallback before the first Fish audio byte only

## What kind of change is this?

Feature, non-breaking and flag-gated.

## Production path

The production TTS leg executes in `packages/cloud/api/v1/voice/session/lib/session.ts`. This is the `VoiceSession` path fed by incremental SSE deltas from `streamElizaConversation`. Provider selection and guarded fallback are wired there, not at a legacy plugin-only seam. Worker outbound WebSocket authorization is wired through `packages/cloud/api/v1/voice/session/lib/provider-socket-factory.ts`.

No workflow changes, check weakening, production deploy, provider spend, or auto-merge.18:T153e,Batch of turn-delivery and sub-agent verify fixes from live operation, sliced to what is not yet on develop:

- **turn delivery floor**: a finished turn always delivers something — deliberate silence is recorded as the chosen terminal, idempotent side effects count as done, `MESSAGE_SENT` fires on actual delivery, and discarded superseded responses record a "replaced" terminal instead of vanishing. New `message.turn-delivery-floor` suite (418 lines) plus side-effect-claim and effects coverage.
- **verify-retry busy-session tolerance**: a verify-retry landing on a busy session no longer false-fails; fail verdicts reach chat instead of dying in the coordinator; Discord gateway loss is observable (session teardown on gateway drop is recorded, not silent). New `verify-retry-busy-session` suite (305 lines).
- **verify-retry stop race**: the router pre-stamps the handoff decision before the successor spawn is awaited, so the original session's teardown `stopped` processed inside the spawn window cannot synthesize a false "stopped before completion" into a room that already got the pass verdict. New `verify-retry-stop-race` suite (324 lines). Superseded parts of the source commit (app-control texts, stage-1 time-in-context) were dropped — develop already carries newer variants.
- **honest trigger replay**: replayed-noop receipts are minted only for dedupeKey-proven duplicates; a legacy instructions+type fuzzy match reports the near-duplicate without claiming verified success; already-exists reminder acks read in voice instead of dedupe-machinery prose; the trajectory terminal stamp uses a distinct `terminal:finished` sentinel so non-turn trajectories never acquire a fabricated planner FINISH; synthetic failure replies cannot seed unanswered-question follow-ups.
- **adjacent test coverage**: pg-manager unit suite for plugin-sql pool wiring / hnsw init / shutdown gating (mocked `pg`, no live Postgres) — coverage that predates this batch and was never upstreamed.

1a:T118e,Second tranche of repair for the `dfdab13ef23` workflow sweep (first: #17790), plus the fallback that makes certification runnable today.

## What

1. **Restores `scripts/vast/` verbatim from `main`** — the sweep deleted it from develop along with the workflows. It contains `local-certify.mjs`, the documented fallback: *"when vast is down or the API key is dead a certifier holding ELIZA_CERT_SIGNING_KEY can produce the same signed certification.json the develop→main gate verifies. Same commands, same output; the gate cannot tell the difference (by design — the signature is what matters)."*
2. **Adds `.github/workflows/certification-hosted.yml`** — a `workflow_dispatch`-only thin caller of `local-certify.mjs` on a GitHub-hosted runner, uploading `certification.json` + the evidence bundle as an artifact.

## Why a hosted fallback is needed now

Dispatching the restored `certification-vast.yml` fails preflight: the repo has `ELIZA_CERT_SIGNING_KEY` but **`VAST_API_KEY` is absent**:

```text
env:
  HAS_VAST_KEY: false
  HAS_SIGNING_KEY: true
##[error]missing repo secret(s): VAST_API_KEY
```

The signing key only exists as a repo secret, so the fallback chain must run on a GitHub runner — hence a workflow.

**No GPU is required for full tier.** The gpu/full-tier vision analyzers resolve their backend from env (`packages/evidence/src/vision-qa/config.ts`): with `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` present (it is, other CI uses it) they call the Anthropic Messages API. The rented vast GPU only ever served the optional local llama-server backend.

## Security posture (mirrors certification-vast)

- `workflow_dispatch` only — no `pull_request` trigger, so PR-controlled code can never see the signing key
- both secrets exposed only to the single step that runs the chain
- `permissions: contents: read`; checkout with `persist-credentials: false`
- signing honesty is enforced by `certify:sign` itself: it refuses to sign mechanically non-pass subjects as pass, so this workflow cannot fabricate a green certification

1b:T148a,## Summary

Telegram's production poller and hosted webhook paths could lose queued updates on restart, collide platform message IDs across chats/bots, split a paired owner back into a connector-derived entity, silently swallow wire failures, or replay an outbound response after an ambiguous send.

This PR makes the vertical restart-safe:

- resolves Telegram senders against the durable canonical owner entity before any room, memory, model, or delivery side effect, and fails closed when the configured identity store is unavailable;
- centralizes owner lookup and unmatched-user fallback identity in one exported Telegram policy consumed by both full and standalone connectors, preventing cutover drift;
- keys message identity by account + chat + Telegram message ID and persists delivery state before wire egress;
- refuses replay after egress begins, reporting an explicit uncertain-delivery failure rather than risking a duplicate;
- stops dropping pending long-poll updates and enforces one process-local poller per bot token across full/standalone modes;
- scopes hosted webhook dedupe by project + bot fingerprint and writes an `egress_started` Redis barrier before Bot API delivery;
- makes connector health depend on actual poller liveness rather than service-object existence.

## Production paths

- Standard connector: `plugin-collector.ts` -> `plugin-telegram/src/index.ts` -> `service.ts` polling/supervision -> `messageManager.ts` identity, canonical ingress, audience-attested core message service, and Telegram egress.
- Standalone opt-in: `plugin-telegram-standalone/src/service.ts` -> `handler.ts`.
- Hosted webhook: `gateway-webhook/src/adapters/telegram.ts` -> `webhook-handler.ts` -> agent-server -> Bot API reply.

No media/rich-text behavior, workflow, check, deployment, or production configuration changed.

## Bidirectional proof

A production-entrypoint harness boots real `AgentRuntime` + plugin-sql/PGlite, creates a durably Telegram-bound canonical owner, drives `MessageManager.handleMessage`, verifies owner-only DM authorization and exactly one wire send, closes the runtime/database, reopens the same PGlite directory, and replays the same Telegram update. The owner row and canonical memory survive while the replay produces zero sends.

The same test copied unchanged (apart from removing the new helper-only readiness assertion/import) onto exact `origin/develop` fails because the inbound memory uses a connector-derived entity instead of the canonical owner (`inbound` is undefined).

## Verification

- Telegram real AgentRuntime/PGlite harness: 3/3 passed
- Telegram focused unit suites: 24/24 passed
- Cross-connector unmatched-user identity regression: passed in the standalone handler suite (both connectors return the same entity ID)
- Exact-head focused recheck: full connector message manager 21/21; standalone handler 3/3; both package typechecks passed
- Telegram full unit package: 120/120 tests passed; one unrelated suite could not import unbuilt `@elizaos/plugin-x402` in this borrowed dependency tree
- Standalone: 9/9 passed + typecheck passed
- Gateway webhook: 37/37 passed + typecheck passed
- Connector health: 2/2 passed
- Full Telegram plugin typecheck passed
- Biome on all changed files passed
- `git diff --check` passed
- Agent package typecheck was attempted; borrowed dependencies lack several optional plugin builds and also emit unchanged orchestrator target diagnostics. No changed health file emitted a diagnostic.

## Safety semantics

Telegram does not accept a caller idempotency key for `sendMessage`. Therefore an ambiguous network outcome cannot be safely retried. The connector writes a durable no-replay barrier immediately before egress. Success becomes `processed`/`delivered`; a crash or error after the barrier remains visible through `runtime.reportError`, logs, or webhook HTTP 503 and is never resent automatically.

# Risks

Medium. This changes authentication caching and money-path timing for generative routes.

- Positive API-key/session decisions have a 60-second physical TTL and refresh after 30 seconds under `waitUntil`; revocation staleness is therefore bounded but non-zero.
- Flat-price media requests use the Cloudflare Durable Object admission lease before provider dispatch, with reconciliation and history persistence deferred.
- Video requests that remain pending preserve the synchronous hold needed by the late-job sweep.
- Independent rollout flags retain rollback paths for combined auth caching and the thin inference entry.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Makes warm LLM, embedding, TTS, STT, image, video, music, SFX, and prompt-generation routes perform one combined remote auth/cache decision before dispatch.
- Removes synchronous Postgres, Railway Redis, pricing-catalog, reservation-ledger, usage-history, and affiliate-payout work from covered warm Worker hot paths.
- Adds flat pricing cache support and flat Durable Object admission for media generation.
- Defers billing reconciliation and generation-history persistence with `waitUntil` after provider output is available.
- Adds a thin Worker entry that lazy-loads only the matched generative route module.
- Enables `INFERENCE_AUTH_CACHE_ENABLED` and `THIN_INFERENCE_ENTRY_ENABLED` in staging and production.
- Documents cache lifetime, cold-cache fail-closed behavior, safety exceptions, and rollback controls.

## What kind of change is this?

Performance improvement and hot-path correctness hardening.

## Why are we doing this?

Warm generative requests were paying avoidable cloud-side latency for repeated authorization/cache work and synchronous database/accounting operations. The target contract is one cache decision, no database access before provider dispatch, and minimal response-tail overhead.

# Documentation changes needed?

Documentation was updated in `packages/cloud/api/docs/inference-hot-path.md`.

1d:T15a0,## What

`app.elizacloud.ai` served the marketing site byte-for-byte — nothing in the codebase branched on the hostname, so the "app" domain was only a second name on the apex build. This adds **app-mode**: on the app hosts, an authenticated visitor lands in *their* dedicated agent instead of the marketing page.

Routing (all gated on the app hostname; the apex is untouched):

| State | Behavior |
|---|---|
| one running dedicated agent | full-page redirect into its web UI via the existing pairing-token flow |
| several running | minimal chooser, most-recently-active first |
| agents exist, none running | Instances view with a resume affordance |
| no agents | the existing "deploy your first agent" flow |
| shared-tier agents only | `chat-home` - shared agents have no per-agent subdomain to pair into, so a pairing call could not succeed |
| not signed in | the existing login flow, returning to the app entry |
| agents fetch fails | falls back to Instances — never a blank screen |

Automatic entry uses `location.replace`, not `assign`: the gate is a transient "connecting" screen, and leaving it in history let Back from the agent UI re-enter it, mint another one-time pairing token, and bounce forward — trapping the user on Firefox/Safari. An explicit chooser pick still pushes so it stays backable.

## Why

The dashboard (funds, instance management) already works end to end. The missing half of the product was the app surface actually *being* the app.

## Scope

6 files under `packages/ui/src/cloud`, +1135/-5. No codegen, no lockfile churn, no API changes — the pairing/auth contracts already existed and are reused as-is.

## Repair after review (`4e73f8dc7cc`)

- **Flag-overrides-apex is now a pinned, recorded decision**: `app-mode.test.ts` asserts `isAppModeHostname("elizacloud.ai", true) === true` with a comment pointing at the build guard as the real invariant.
- **Production builds hard-fail if a forced host-mode flag is set**: new `packages/app/scripts/forced-host-mode-guard.mjs` wired as a vite plugin - any production-mode build (all four Pages `build:web` invocations in `cloud-cf-deploy.yml`, prod and staging, console and app) throws at `configResolved` when `VITE_FORCE_APP_MODE` or `VITE_FORCE_APEX_CONSOLE` is set (loadEnv-aware, so `.env` files are covered). Dev server and `--mode development` keep the escape hatch. A misconfigured deploy now fails loudly at build time instead of silently turning the apex into a redirect gate.
- **Fixed a latent test break on the reviewed head**: the replace-instead-of-push commit stubbed only `assign`, so jsdom's unimplemented `location.replace` failed both automatic-entry pairing tests; both stubs now land in the same log, and the replace/push split is itself pinned in `app-mode.test.ts`.

- filler completions: the planner produces a contentless acknowledgement in a turn nobody addressed to the agent

- `ambient_turn_policy` planner instruction gated on the structural addressed-turn classifier, never on message text

- task-history planner-facing result texts rewritten machine-shaped ("Report the count to the user in your own words") so a protocol-failure echo cannot pass as a chat reply, and the stale in-file comment claiming the deterministic fallback relays `result.text` corrected

20:T3547,Closes #17763

## What was broken

`resolveIdentity` in the webhook gateway threw whenever the identity resolve API answered `agentId: null`. That answer is correct — it means "known cloud account, no sandbox yet" — and the gateway was the only thing treating it as a malformed response. Because it threw instead of returning, the handler's `if (!identity)` onboarding branch was unreachable for those senders, and the throw landed in the fire-and-forget `processMessage().catch(...)` **after** the webhook had already acked 200. Telegram does not retry a 200, so the sender got silence.

Measured on production, 2026-08-05 — every linked connector identity is in exactly that state:

| platform | linked identities | of those, org owns zero sandboxes |
|---|---|---|
| telegram | 11 | **11** |
| whatsapp | 0 | 0 |
| phone (twilio + blooio) | 31 | **31** |

31 distinct organizations. The shared webhook has never worked for anyone who signed up without already owning an agent.

## What changed

**`ResolvedIdentity.agentId` is optional**, and the guard only rejects a response missing `userId`/`organizationId` — the fields whose absence really is a broken response. The `null` on the wire is normalised once, at the boundary, with no cast.

**Routing keys on "is there an agent that can serve this message"** — the sandbox row *and* its `agent:<id>:server` registry key — not on `agentId != null`. The row appears the instant provisioning starts; the key only once a container has booted and heartbeated. Branching on the row alone would answer message 1 and then go silent again until boot, permanently if provisioning ends in `error`. A per-agent webhook URL (`/webhook/:project/:platform/:agentId`) keeps precedence verbatim, including its log-and-return when no server is registered — diverting one of its senders to personal onboarding would run that flow on somebody else's bot.

The decision never reads `sandbox.status`. A stopped agent is still a resolved agent; re-onboarding one would provision a duplicate. The single line preventing that is the early return on an existing sandbox in `ensureElizaAppProvisioning`.

**An identity with no agent is not cached.** The 300s identity cache would otherwise keep serving the pre-agent answer well after the agent exists. Only settled identities are cached, which also means there is nothing to invalidate afterwards.

**The onboarding route resolves the cloud account itself** from `(platform, platformUserId)` on the internal branch — the direction `agent-gateway-router.ts` already takes for Discord. It never accepts an account named in the request body. `requireInternalAuth` is a flat shared secret with no per-service scoping (its `service` field is read by nobody), and this path reaches `launchManagedElizaAgent`, whose `launchUrl` carries a `cloudLaunchSession` redeemable **with no authentication** for the agent's bearer token at `v1/eliza/launch-sessions/[sessionId]`. A body-claimed `organizationId` would hand that to every internal-secret holder at once, including the internet-facing Railway gateway.

The provider is required rather than optional for the same reason: `resolveIdentity` without one falls back to sniffing the identifier's shape and would match a UUID, an email or a wallet address — a wider claim than "this is telegram user X", the only claim a gateway can make.

**The internal branch no longer receives `launchUrl`, `controlPanelUrl` or the transcript.** No gateway reads them — `webhook-handler.ts` reads only `body.data.reply`. The browser hook (session-authed) is unaffected and keeps the full payload.

**`|| config.agentId` is gone** from the routing expression, along with `WebhookConfig.agentId` and the `DEFAULT_AGENT_ID` lookup that fed it. It was dead by construction (it could only fire when `identity.agentId` was falsy, the case now diverted) and non-functional anyway: it holds an `agents`-table id while the registry is keyed by sandbox id, so it could never have resolved a server.

## Behaviour after the fix

Telegram or WhatsApp with a Latin display name: message 1 gets a real reply and starts provisioning; later messages keep getting honest status replies instead of silence; once the container registers, messages route to the user's own agent. Twilio and blooio need two turns by design — the display name is the phone number, so the user must actually answer "what should I call you?".

## Evidence

**Blast radius, measured before shipping** (read-only against production): 31 organizations provision a container and consume the $5 starter grant on their next inbound message. `checkAgentCreditGate` is the only brake and it only stops drained orgs. It also re-provisions anyone who deliberately deleted their agent.

**Known gate, stated rather than discovered later:** `ensureElizaAppProvisioning` runs only once a preferred name is captured, and sanitisation keeps printable ASCII only. A Telegram `first_name` that is Cyrillic, CJK or emoji-only sanitises to empty, and both name-inference patterns are ASCII — that user is asked "what should I call you?" forever unless they type a Latin-script name. Pre-existing, out of scope here, filed in the issue.

## Deploy note

This service ships via Railway, not the Worker pipeline. It needs an explicit `railway redeploy --service gateway-webhook` — a build that does not reach an already-started process is what made the Redis fix on this same service appear to fail twice on 2026-08-05.

Manual retest is also misleading for five minutes at a time: the dedup key is written before the background work with a 300s TTL, so re-sending the same text is swallowed as a duplicate. Vary the message.

[stan-cloud]


## Second round — what the review changed

An adversarial read of the first commit found three things worth fixing, all now in.

**An agent that never booted and an agent whose pod is down are not the same state, and collapsing them made the fix lie to established owners.** `agent:<id>:server` is written by a booted container and lives 30 days; `server:<name>:url` is refreshed by the pod heartbeat and expires after 120s. So a missing routing key means "never came up" — the case this PR exists for — and a present routing key with no URL means an established agent that is down or scaled to zero. The first commit sent both to onboarding, which would have answered that owner *"You're live — your private agent is running"* while the message went nowhere, and copied the onboarding transcript into their agent's memory a second time. `resolveAgentServer` now returns which of the two it found; only `unregistered` onboards.

**The gateway now sends `Idempotency-Key: <messageId>`.** The onboarding route and its session coordinator both keep a replay ledger on that header and nothing was using it. The only protection against platform redelivery was the 5-minute Redis dedup key; a retry outside that window appended the message to the transcript twice and re-entered provisioning. That existed before, but it applied to unlinked strangers only — this PR makes the onboarding path the norm for every linked-but-agentless user, so the exposure widens materially.

**`loginUrl` joins the browser-only fields.** It is built from the session-continuation token, which resolves back onto a platform session — the same class of credential as the `cloudLaunchSession` in `launchUrl`, stripped by the same argument. No gateway reads it, and when the user genuinely needs the link it is already inside `reply`.

Plus three honesty fixes: a wire type that said `agentId?: string` on the branch production actually takes, making the `null` normalisation — the one value this PR is about — invisible to the compiler; a comment of mine that claimed per-agent precedence protects senders it never reaches (a sender with no cloud account is onboarded by the branch above, per-agent URL or not, and that predates this change); and a shared JSDoc that described one caller's behaviour as the helper's contract while contradicting the other caller shipped alongside it.

Three branches the review showed were unpinned now have tests — the unreachable-pod case, the per-agent webhook whose bound agent has no server, and the identity cache write itself — plus a `resolveIdentity` suite for the narrowed guard. Thirteen pins in total, each mutation-verified.

## Found and deliberately not done here

- `packages/cloud/api/internal/webhook/config/route.ts` still returns an `agentId` that the gateway now ignores. Harmless dead payload (the caller sent that id as the query param), but it is cached into Redis for 300s. Left alone to keep this diff on the bug.
- `elizaAppConfig.defaultAgentId` (`packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/eliza-app/config.ts`) has no readers repo-wide. Already dead before this change — the gateway read the env var directly — so removing it belongs to its own sweep.
- `ensureElizaAppStarterCredits` is a read-then-write outside the org advisory lock. Two genuinely concurrent cross-session first turns are stopped from double-granting by the unique index on the deterministic credit key, but the loser throws, which surfaces as a 500 on `/onboarding/chat` and one silent message. Narrow, pre-existing, self-heals on the next message — but it is the same symptom this PR exists to remove, so it is written down rather than left to be rediscovered.

## Rebased onto develop after #17746

#17746 landed on develop first and covers part of the same journey: nullable `agentId`, the linked-but-agentless early return, and a 15s transient TTL with the 404 negative-cache write. Rebased and squashed onto `9509cb36786`; this PR is now the delta on top of it.

**What is still only here:** the `unregistered`/`unreachable` split (#17746 still log-and-returns silence for a sender whose sandbox row exists but whose container never registered — the whole boot window, and permanently on a provisioning error), `Idempotency-Key` on the onboarding POST, the API-side account resolution and response redaction, and the `DEFAULT_AGENT_ID` removal.

**Policy taken from develop, against my earlier position:** agentless and 404 identities keep the 15s transient TTL. An earlier revision of this branch cached neither, for instant cutover when the agent appears. That does not survive the clock — a container cannot boot inside 15 seconds, so the stale window costs nothing user-visible while the cache saves a resolver round trip on every inbound message from the 31 organizations this change puts through onboarding.

21:T1b16,Production agent nodes have no local embedding sidecar and every agent runs on the pure cloud-embedding path. This adds the sidecar to node provisioning, surfaces its absence in node health, and self-heals nodes that lost it.

## Verified fleet state

All 8 production agent nodes were checked directly — running containers, stopped containers, pulled images, and systemd units:

| Hostname | Agent containers | Sidecar |
|---|---|---|
| eliza-prod-robot-5 | 34 healthy | none |
| eliza-prod-robot-3 | 28 healthy | none |
| eliza-prod-robot-2 | 33 healthy | none |
| eliza-prod-robot-4 | 38 healthy | none |
| eliza-prod-robot-6 | 28 healthy | none |
| eliza-core-3702b6f8 | 4 healthy | none |
| eliza-core-c5830c06 | 4 healthy | none |
| eliza-core-7226e98b | 4 healthy | none |

**Zero of 8** have a sidecar in any form. `docker inspect` across ~170 agent containers shows `ELIZAOS_CLOUD_EMBEDDING_URL=https://api.elizacloud.ai`, `EMBEDDING_DIMENSION=1536`, and no `EMBEDDING_BASE_URL`.

## Root cause

The sidecar has only ever existed as hand-installed state — **no provisioning path installs one**. Autoscaled nodes boot from `buildContainerNodeUserData` (`node-bootstrap.ts`); operator boxes come up via `onboard-docker-node.ts`. Neither mentions it, and the repo contained zero references to `text-embeddings-inference`. Two mechanisms then erase hand-installs: autoscaled nodes are rebuilt from user-data that never had it, and the disk-reclaim cycle prunes unlabelled containers. That is why this regresses after every infra churn, and why "restored on 3 nodes" does not persist.

## Change

- **New `containers/embedding-sidecar.ts`** — single source of truth: container `eliza-embedding-sidecar`, pinned `ghcr.io/huggingface/text-embeddings-inference:cpu-1.8` serving `thenlper/gte-small`, shared bridge network, loopback-only health port, model-cache bind mount, `--restart always`, and the `ai.elizaos.managed-by=eliza-cloud` label that makes it **prune-immune**. Pure builders; shell-unsafe config fails fast; all overridable via `CONTAINERS_EMBEDDING_SIDECAR_*`.
- **Bootstrap installs it** — cloud-init runs the idempotent ensure command after network setup and ghcr stale-credential cleanup (the same `denied`-pull hazard applies to this image). `onboard-docker-node.ts` gains the identical step, non-fatal.
- **Health surfaces and self-heals it** — `healthCheckNode` probes `/health` after `docker info` answers, persists the verdict to `docker_nodes.metadata.embeddingSidecar`, exposes it on `NodeCapacityReport`, ERROR-logs absence, and reinstalls with a 30-minute per-node cooldown (kill-switch `CONTAINERS_EMBEDDING_SIDECAR_SELF_HEAL=false`). The verdict never owns node status.

**The self-heal is the convergence path for the existing 8 nodes**: deploying the control plane installs sidecars fleet-wide on the next health cycle, with no hand-installs.

## Deliberately out of scope

Flipping agent containers to consume the sidecar. `applyManagedAgentInferenceEnvDefaults` pins 1536-dim cloud embeddings; a hot switch to 384-dim degrades recall until existing stores are re-embedded (#9911 class). That cutover belongs on the existing per-agent flag lane, after this lands.

# Sync with develop

- [x] Branched from current `origin/develop`; no conflicts.

# Risks

Low, and bounded by construction. The turn body is untouched — `handleMessage` becomes a thin wrapper that registers with the registry and delegates to the unchanged implementation. The only behavioural change outside shutdown is one registry insert per turn. `stop()` gains a bounded await (10s ceiling) before teardown that previously did not exist; a hang there is impossible by construction, and the abandon path is logged, not silent.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

`DiscordService.stop()` destroyed debouncers, managers, and clients immediately, with nothing tracking in-flight `handleMessage` calls — so shutting down mid-turn tore the client out from under the turn, and the status reaction that turn had applied stayed showing "in progress" forever. Each reaction controller was a bare closure inside `handleMessage`, so shutdown had no handle to reconcile with even if it had waited.

- A turn registry (`shutdown-drain.ts`) tracks each in-flight turn and the status-reaction controller it drives.
- `stop()` awaits that drain **before** any teardown, bounded by `DISCORD_SHUTDOWN_DRAIN_TIMEOUT_MS` (10s). No unbounded wait: blocking process shutdown indefinitely is worse than abandoning loudly.
- When the bound elapses, each still-running turn's reaction is forced to its terminal marker and the abandonment is logged with structured context (observed turns, abandoned message ids, the timeout).
- `StatusReactionController` gains `whenFinished` and `abandon()`, reusing its existing terminal transition — purely additive.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking).

## Deliberately not claimed by this PR

Naming these so #16318 does not look closed by a partial fix:

- **No inbound-ingress cordon.** discord.js keeps delivering gateway events until the client is destroyed, so a turn that *starts* after `drain()` begins is not covered. A cordon belongs to the runtime-level shutdown path, not this connector. Documented in `shutdown-drain.ts`'s header.
- **No supervisor-chain change.** `dev-ui` sends SIGKILL 1.5s after SIGTERM — shorter than any drain this plugin can offer. Making the 10s drain effective end-to-end requires a change outside `plugins/plugin-discord`.
- **No startup reconciliation** of reactions stranded by a prior hard kill.

# Documentation changes needed?

No — behaviour restored to what the connector's own contract already implied.

23:Tf54,Reasoning models burn completion tokens deliberating before emitting the required tool call. With `toolChoice: required` and a 1024-token completion budget, an ambiguous ask exhausts the whole budget on reasoning deltas and the provider terminates the stream with an in-stream server error that is indistinguishable from a transient 5xx — reproducing 100% per-ask. Wire capture shows the entire budget consumed by reasoning with no tool call emitted.

- chat planner completion budget 1024 → 4096, with an `ELIZA_PLANNER_MAX_TOKENS` override for operators who need to tune it; same failure class as the coding lane's earlier budget fix (#10132)
- live stream-start retry widened 3 → 5 attempts so a sustained burst of real provider 500s at stream start still recovers; pinned by a sustained-burst test (five failures, sixth attempt delivers), while the no-retry-after-first-token contract stays intact

Tests: planner-loop suite (111) and stream-start-retry shape suite (11) green; typecheck clean on core + plugin-openai.

24:T8f9,## Why

eliza.app was switched to a graceful waitlist on 2026-08-05 (Cloudflare Pages deployment `d562e1eb-9c39-4644-9b8b-4f53fea90257`, project `eliza-app-home`) because the previous `packages/homepage` build exposed a stale phone/channel demo in production: hard-coded `+14159611510`, client-only mock OTP, and download routes pointing at dead surfaces.

That swap went out via direct wrangler upload, which left a **resurrection hole**: `Deploy Homepage` triggers on any develop push touching `packages/homepage/**` and would rebuild the stale Vite app over the live waitlist.

## What

- Upstreams the exact deployed artifact into `packages/homepage/waitlist/site` — **byte-identical to production** (sha256 of every file matches the deploy receipt's artifact sums)
- Includes the Pages Function (`functions/api/waitlist.js`: validation, hashed KV keys, dedupe, IP rate-limit, origin check, honeypot) and its 4 unit tests (passing)
- Points `Deploy Homepage` at the waitlist directory; wrangler deploys `site/` with the adjacent `functions/` picked up from cwd

## ⚠️ Workflow changes (flagging for review)

`.github/workflows/deploy-homepage.yml` is substantially rewritten:
- Deploy source: `packages/homepage` vite build → `packages/homepage/waitlist` static artifact
- Adds artifact-completeness gate + an explicit guard that fails the build if the stale demo phone number appears in deploy artifacts
- `permissions: contents: write` → `read` (write was only used to auto-commit visual snapshot baselines for the old suite)
- Trigger paths narrowed to the waitlist directory + the workflow itself
- The old vite build/e2e/visual steps are removed from the **deploy pipeline only**; the full app, its tests, and CI coverage elsewhere are untouched and it remains in-tree for the relaunch

Restoring the full app to production becomes an explicit revert of this workflow, not a side effect of a push.

## Ops receipts

- Live prod: `d562e1eb` (waitlist), rollback anchor: `088b3bd5` (pre-swap deployment, one-click Pages rollback, no DNS involved)
- KV binding `WAITLIST` → `eliza-app-waitlist-production` (`c776f8d7…`) already exists on the Pages project (both envs)
- No deploy is triggered by this PR; first CI deploy happens on merge and reproduces the already-live surface25:T1371,## Summary

Stops programmatic and runtime-internal traffic from creating user-facing notification cards.

This fixes the LP3 home-screen spam reported from the production Android WebView:

- `New API message` / `Show all 27 Agent Message Api notifications`
- `Show all 3 Compat Openai notifications`
- `Show all 2 Trigger notifications`
- bench payloads such as `bench bench 3: reply with one short sentence`
- internal system automation failures (for example the Smithers/device-health error card)

Real human connector traffic, including Discord DMs, still creates notifications.

## Root causes

### 1. Inbound message classification was allow-by-default

`bridgeMessageReceivedToStreams()` in `packages/core/src/services/agent-event-bridge.ts` is the upstream producer for message notifications. Its small denylist excluded only local source names such as `client_chat`, `api`, and `web`. It therefore treated these as human connector traffic:

- `agent_message_api`, used by `POST /agents/:id/message` and the bench harness
- `compat_openai` / `compat_anthropic`
- `trigger-prompt`, `sub_agent`, `coding-agent`, `agent_greeting`
- API calls with a caller-chosen source/platform name

### 2. Trigger outcomes notified unconditionally

`packages/agent/src/triggers/runtime.ts` persists every trigger's `lastStatus`, `lastError`, and run history for the Automations UI, but it also minted completion/failure inbox records on every execution. System health checks therefore leaked implementation activity into the Home rail as `Trigger` stacks.

The UI notification center was faithfully rendering bad upstream records. Hiding those rows in the UI would leave persisted notifications, badge counts, and OS delivery wrong.

## Fix

- Add a canonical non-notifying source set for local, API/compat, and runtime-internal message sources.
- Add a channel-level backstop: all API routes stamp `channelType: API`, so arbitrary caller-chosen source labels still cannot masquerade as human connector traffic.
- Default trigger outcome notifications off. Durable status/error remains in the Automations UI, and prompt-automation replies still deliver normally.
- Preserve the old trigger rail only as an explicit diagnostic opt-in via runtime setting or environment:

```text
ELIZA_DEBUG_TRIGGER_NOTIFICATIONS=true
```

## Bidirectional proof

```sh
bun run --cwd packages/core vitest run src/services/agent-event-bridge.test.ts
bun run --cwd packages/agent vitest run src/triggers/runtime.test.ts
```

- Core on `develop` with only the new test: **fails**, because API/compat/custom API-channel/trigger-prompt messages mint notifications. This branch: **15/15 passed**.
- Trigger runtime on `develop` with only the new test: **fails**, because a success + failure create two inbox records. This branch: **21/21 passed**.
- Opposite direction:
  - a real Discord inbound message still creates exactly one `New discord message from alice` notification
  - debug opt-in still emits both success and failure trigger diagnostics

## Production path

These are the runtime producers consumed by `GET /api/notifications` and the `agent_event` notification stream. The Android cloud app is built by `packages/app-core/scripts/run-mobile-build.mjs android-cloud-debug`; `packages/app/src/main.tsx` mounts `@elizaos/ui/App`, whose notification store hydrates and renders those records. Fixing producers removes invalid rows from every surface rather than only hiding the LP3 list.

<!-- attribution-row:status -->
- Attribution status: `self-reported`26:T2054,## Summary

Closes #16394.

Surfaces hidden reasoning-token usage (`completion_tokens_details.reasoning_tokens`) on inference spans so a Cerebras/OpenAI reasoning burst is attributable per model call, and bounds the last unbounded turn-critical helper (`generateFailureReplyText`) on reasoning models.

On Cerebras reasoning models (zai-glm-4.7, gpt-oss-120b), hidden reasoning tokens dominate tail latency — live Stage-1 latencies on the same model/prompt ranged 1.2s → 12.8s, with direct-API measurement showing ~400 hidden reasoning tokens of 407 completion tokens. The OpenAI adapter already captured `outputTokenDetails.reasoningTokens` / `usage.reasoningTokens` in its local usage object, but `@elizaos/core` `TokenUsage` could not represent the field, `AgentRuntime` did not propagate it into the model-call span, and `generateFailureReplyText` still tried four text model slots with only `{prompt}`.

### Ask 1 — surface reasoning tokens on inference spans

- Add an optional finite non-negative `reasoningTokens?` to the core `TokenUsage` type (`packages/core/src/types/model.ts`). Missing stays missing — never zero — so an unattributed burst is distinguishable from a confirmed-none call (thinking=off returns a real attributable `0`).
- Thread the field through every type that mirrors token usage: the trajectory recorder (`RecordedUsage`, `RecordedTrajectoryMetrics.totalReasoningTokens`), the trajectory service (`LLMCall`, `TrajectoryLlmCallRecord`, `TrajectoryLlmCallDetails`, `TrajectoryRuntimeLlmCallParams`), the export path (`ElizaNativeModelResponseRecord`), and the `MODEL_USED` event payload.
- `logModelCall` reads reasoning tokens from the successful model result via the new `readReasoningTokensFromResponse` helper (covers `usage.reasoningTokens` plus the `providerMetadata.completion_tokens_details.reasoning_tokens` / camelCase fallbacks) and attaches them to the `model:*` inference span meta. Covers both streaming and non-streaming return paths that funnel through `logModelCall`.
- The OpenAI adapter's `convertUsage` already normalized the field; `emitModelUsageEvent` now emits it on `MODEL_USED`.
- The trajectory recorder rolls `reasoningTokens` into per-call usage, the per-turn `totalReasoningTokens` metric, and the markdown report (summary + per-stage lines).
- `recordUseModelTrajectory` propagates `reasoningTokens` into the trajectory LLM-call record, and `normalizeLlmCall` persists it.

### Ask 2 — bound the residual unbounded call site

The triage confirmed Stage-1, the evaluator, every planner iteration, callback rewrites, and API fallback/direct rewrites already set `thinking=off`. The confirmed residual was `generateFailureReplyText`, which tried four text model slots (`TEXT_LARGE`, `RESPONSE_HANDLER`, `TEXT_SMALL`, `TEXT_NANO`) with only `{prompt}`. It now carries `providerOptions.eliza.thinking = "off"` on every fallback slot, matching the other turn-critical helpers. Without it a drained/failed turn could still spend hundreds of hidden reasoning tokens before producing visible text.

```
bun run --cwd packages/core typecheck
bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-openai typecheck
bun run --cwd packages/core test -- reasoning-token-telemetry inference-timing message-failure-reply trajectory-recorder
```
